❯ I need you to help me design an R package called floaties. Do not write any   
  code or create the package skeleton, this is just a research and planning     
  phase.                                                                        
  The core requirements of this package is that it allows the users to create   
  waterfall and swimlane plots for medical data review. Although, we want the   
  users to choose which graphing engine they want: ggplot2, plotly, or          
  echarts4r to create these two plots. I also want the functions to accept      
  domain agnostic datasets as long as they follow certain pre-defined structure 
  so we can just take in the dataset name, the columns and apply tidyeval to    
  do any plot related data manipulation we need, this makes sure that our       
  package works with SDTM/ADaM/any other format as long as they structure it in 
  the way we want them to. Eventually, we may expand to other plots too, but    
  for now let's just focus on these two. Once you're done with the research I   
  also want you to create a CLAUDE.md so it helps with my R package development 
  workflow, I want to make sure that everytime a feature is complete R CMD      
  check is run and we don't have any errors. I also want to follow the          
  tidyverse style guide.                                                        

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● I've done the research and written CLAUDE.md. No package code or skeleton
  exists yet. The plan is below; the open questions at the end need your call
  before the skeleton phase.

  What already exists

  - {osprey} (Roche/teal) has g_waterfall() and g_swimlane(), but they only draw
    with ggplot2. They also take bare vectors, not a data frame plus column
    names. You'd pass bar_id = adsl$USUBJID, bar_height = ..., which makes them
    hard to use with tidyverse code and ties them to one data layout. That's the
    gap floaties fills.
  - {ggswim} and {swimplot}: swimmer plots that only draw with ggplot2.
  - For examples, {pharmaverseadam} (installed here) has realistic ADaM data:
    adtr_onco (the SDIAM parameter with PCHG gives the waterfall), adrs_onco
    (response events for swimlane markers) and adsl (treatment duration and
    arm).

  Proposed design

  1. Three stages for every plot: validate → prepare → render.
  - prep_waterfall() / prep_swimlane() do all the tidy-evaluation work: renaming
    to standard internal columns, sorting, capping and setting factor levels.
    They return a floaties_spec: a tibble plus metadata (labels, colours,
    shapes, reference lines). They don't depend on any graphing engine.
  - render_<plot>_<engine>() only turns a spec into graphics.
  - Adding a plot means one validate/prepare stage plus three renderers. Adding
    an engine means one renderer per plot.
  - Exporting prep_*() also lets reviewers check the exact data behind a plot,
    which helps with QC and validation.

  2. User-facing API. One function per plot, with engine as the last argument:
  plot_waterfall(data, subject, value, fill = NULL, facet = NULL, tooltip =
  NULL,
                 sort = c("desc", "asc", "none"), ref_lines = c(20, -30), cap =
  NULL,
                 colors = NULL, engine = c("ggplot2", "plotly", "echarts4r"))

  plot_swimlane(data, subject, end, start = NULL, fill = NULL, ongoing = NULL,
                events = NULL, event_time = NULL, event = NULL,
                sort = ..., colors = NULL, shapes = NULL, engine = ...)
  - Single columns use {{ }}. The tooltip argument takes several columns via
    tidyselect.
  - Default axis and legend labels come from the column's label attribute, which
    ADaM data usually has, and otherwise from the column name.

  3. Data contracts (also written in CLAUDE.md):
  - Waterfall: one row per subject. If a subject appears twice, it stops with a
    classed error that tells the user to filter first.
  - Swimlane: two tables. One row per subject for the lanes (like ADSL), and an
    optional events table with many rows per subject (like ADRS). Real data
    comes in exactly this shape, so it avoids forcing users into awkward joins.
  - floaties never derives clinical values such as best % change. It only does
    plot-related manipulation. That's what keeps it domain-agnostic.

  4. Engine details.
  - ggplot2 is a required dependency (Imports) and the default engine.
  - plotly and echarts4r are optional dependencies (Suggests), checked with
    rlang::check_installed(), because plotly pulls in a lot of packages.
  - plotly plots are built with plot_ly() directly, not ggplotly(). ggplotly()
    handles arrows, custom tooltips and shape mapping poorly.
  - A shared internal lookup keeps colours, marker shapes and legend order the
    same across engines. Each engine names its shapes differently (ggplot2 shape
    codes, plotly symbols, echarts symbols), so a mapping layer is needed.
  - Each engine returns its own native object (a ggplot, or an htmlwidget for
    plotly and echarts4r), so users can keep customising it and it works in
    Shiny.

  5. Testing.
  - The prepare stage gets the most tests, with plain expect_equal() checks.
  - Error messages get snapshot tests.
  - ggplot2 output gets vdiffr image comparisons.
  - plotly and echarts4r output gets checks on the widget's internal structure
    instead of fragile image comparisons.
  - Every contract rule is also tested with non-CDISC column names, to prove the
    domain-agnostic promise.

  About CLAUDE.md

  It covers the architecture, data contracts, tidy-evaluation rules, tidyverse
  style (checked with styler and lintr), commands, and a mandatory definition of
  done ending in devtools::check(). Two things to know:
  - I set the bar higher than you asked: 0 errors and 0 warnings, and any
    remaining NOTE must be explained, because CRAN treats warnings as blocking.
    Say if you want errors only.
  - It lists .venv, graft/, .claude/ and .mcp.json for .Rbuildignore. Your repo
    has these, and they would trigger R CMD check NOTEs.

  CLAUDE.md guides Claude but can't force anything. If you want R CMD check
  guaranteed to run, I can add a hook that runs it automatically.

  Open questions

  1. Swimlane input: two data frames (recommended), or a single long table?
  2. Function names: plot_waterfall() / prep_waterfall() (recommended), or a
     package prefix like fl_waterfall()?
  3. Time values: numeric only for v1 (recommended), or also accept Date columns
     plus a reference date?
  4. Release target: CRAN or internal? This affects the minimum R version, the
     licence and how strict to be about NOTEs.

● I've cut CLAUDE.md from about 130 lines to about 35. It now has four sections:
  overview, architecture, conventions and definition of done.

  What I removed:

  - Detailed testing and dependency rules: trimmed or folded into short
    conventions.

    into .Rbuildignore when we set up the package.

  Check rule: it now requires only 0 errors, as you originally asked, instead of
  the stricter "0 errors and 0 warnings" I'd added.

● The waterfall plot is built and works in all three engines. R CMD check passes
  with 0 errors, 0 warnings and 1 NOTE. All 65 tests pass and lint is clean. I
  looked at the ggplot2 image, and opened the plotly and echarts4r versions in a
  browser to check the bars, reference lines, capped labels and tooltips.

  Dependencies: plotly and echarts4r are now required dependencies (Imports),
  along with cli, tidyselect, htmltools and htmlwidgets. CLAUDE.md is updated to
  match.

  What you can call
  - plot_waterfall() takes data, subject, value, fill, tooltip, sort, cap,
    ref_lines, colors, title, x_label, y_label and engine. It returns the
    engine's own object (a ggplot or an htmlwidget).
  - prep_waterfall() returns the prepared data the plot is drawn from, for QC.
    It has a print method.
  - tumor_change is a 40-row synthetic example dataset with non-CDISC column
    names. It's generated by a script in data-raw/.

  Behaviour
  - Columns can be given as bare names or strings. Axis titles default to the
    column's label attribute, which ADaM and SDTM usually have.
  - data must have one row per subject. If a subject appears twice, or a subject
    ID is missing, it stops with an error.
  - Rows with a missing value are dropped with a warning. Missing fill values
    show as a grey "Missing" group.
  - cap draws values above the cap at the cap and labels them with their real
    value. Reference lines default to the RECIST thresholds of +20 and -30.
  - The default colours are the colourblind-friendly Okabe-Ito palette, and each
    fill level gets the same colour in every engine.

  Tests cover the data preparation, every error message, image comparisons for
  ggplot2, and the widget contents for plotly and echarts4r (order, colours,
  reference lines, capped labels). The README now shows the waterfall with an
  example image and a pharmaverseadam example, which I ran to confirm it works.
  NEWS is updated too.

  Decisions to confirm
  - Faceting: I left out facet for now. It works very differently in each engine
    and would have doubled the work. It's easy to add later.
  - cap direction: it only limits large positive values. Decreases already stop
    at -100%.
  - Subject IDs on the x-axis are hidden, since reviewers usually read them from
    the tooltip. You can show them by customising the returned object.

  Remaining NOTE and tooling: the leftover "unable to verify current time" NOTE
  comes from this machine, not the package. lintr reports false warnings unless
  the package is installed first, so I added an install step to the CLAUDE.md
  command list.

  Nothing is committed yet, and everything is still on main. Should I create a
  branch and commit the scaffold and waterfall, maybe as two commits, before
  starting on the swimlane?

      163 +  vapply(

      171 +                           levels,                            
      172 +                           arg = rlang::caller_arg(shapes),   
      173 +                           call = rlang::caller_env()) {

      184 +        call = call                                           
      185 +      )                                                       
      186 +    }                                                         
      187 +    return(rlang::set_names(known[seq_along(levels)], levels))
      188 +  }                                                           
      189 +                                                              
      190 +  out <- resolve_level_values(shapes, levels, "shape", arg = a
          +rg, call = call)

      192 +  if (length(unknown) > 0) {

      201 +    )                                                         
      202 +  }

        2 -# floaties                                                    
        3 -                                                              
        4 -<!-- badges: start -->

        7 -                                                              
        8 -floaties creates **waterfall** and **swimlane** plots for medi
          -cal and clinical                                              
        9 -data review. You choose the graphics engine: **ggplot2** for s
          -tatic,                                                        
       10 -publication-ready figures, or **plotly** or **echarts4r** for 
          -interactive                                                   
       11 -widgets in reports and Shiny apps.                            
       12 -                                                              
       13 -floaties works with any data standard. It doesn't expect SDTM,
          - ADaM, or any                                                 
       14 -particular variable names. You pass a data frame and point flo
          -aties to the                                                  
       15 -columns it needs using tidy evaluation. If your data has the r
          -ight structure,                                               
       16 -floaties plots it.                                            
       17 -                                                              
       18 -> floaties is in early development. The waterfall plot is avai
          -lable; the

       22 -

       26 -# install.packages("pak")                                     
       27 -pak::pak("vedhav/floaties")                                   
       28 -```                                                           
       29 -                                                              
       30 -## Waterfall plot                                             
       31 -                                                              
       32 -Shows one bar per subject, sorted by value. A typical use is t
          -he best percent                                               
       33 -change from baseline in tumor size, colored by best overall re
          -sponse, with                                                  
       34 -dashed reference lines at +20% and -30% (RECIST 1.1).         
       35 -                                                              
       36 -**Data structure:** one row per subject, with a subject identi
          -fier column and a                                             
       37 -numeric value column. An optional `fill` column colors the bar
          -s, and                                                        
       38 -`tooltip` adds extra columns to the hover text in interactive 
          -engines.                                                      
       39 -floaties doesn't derive the value for you: filter or summarize
          - your data to

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● The swimlane plot is built and works in all three engines. R CMD check passes
  with 0 errors, 0 warnings and 1 NOTE (the same "unable to verify current time"
  NOTE from this machine). All 146 tests pass and lint is clean. I looked at
  the ggplot2 image and opened the plotly and echarts4r versions in a browser,
  including tooltips and a case with negative start times. I also ran it on real
  ADaM data from pharmaverseadam.

  How it works
  - plot_swimlane() takes two tables: data with one row per subject for the
    lanes, and an optional events table with any number of rows per subject for
    the markers.
    - Lanes: start (0 if not given), end, fill, ongoing (logical, or "Y"/"N"
      flags like ADaM uses) and extra tooltip columns.
    - Events: event_time and event. If the events table names its subject column
      differently, point to it with event_subject.
    - Also: sort (longest lane at the top by default), vertical ref_lines,
      colors, event_colors, event_shapes, and the same title and axis-label
      options as the waterfall.
  - prep_swimlane() returns the prepared lanes and events for QC.
  - Ongoing lanes end with a short arrow and get an "Ongoing" legend entry in
    every engine.
  - Marker shapes come from a fixed set of six: circle, square, triangle,
    diamond, cross and star. Each looks the same in all three engines. If an
    event column has more than six types, you get a clear error unless you pick
    the shapes yourself.
  - Lane colors are lightened versions of the default palette, so event markers
    stand out on top of them.
  - Data problems behave like the waterfall:
    - Missing times and events for subjects not in data are dropped with a
      warning.
    - Duplicate subjects, or a lane that ends before it starts, stop with an
      error.
  - Negative start times (for example screening before day 0) work in every
    engine. echarts can't draw that with stacked bars, so its lanes use a custom
    series.
  - New example datasets: treatment_duration and response_events.

  Changes that also touch the waterfall
  - Default colour order: the third default colour is now green instead of sky
    blue, because sky-blue markers were hard to see on blue lanes. In the
    waterfall, only the third group changes (SD in the README example).
  - Shared messages: the color error messages are now shared by both plots. That
    fixed a bug where one read "at least 3 color".
  - Code: the waterfall and swimlane share one set of helpers for colors and
    shapes.

  Docs: the README now has a swimlane section with an image, a custom-shapes
  example and a checked pharmaverseadam example. The waterfall image is
  regenerated, and NEWS is updated.

  Two things to know
  - Overlapping markers: events at exactly the same time overlap. For example,
    PD and Death on the same day draw on top of each other, though the tooltips
    show both. I didn't add any automatic nudging.
  - No faceting: as with the waterfall, faceting is left out for now.

  Everything, including the scaffold and waterfall work, is still uncommitted on
  main. Should I create a branch and commit it, maybe as separate scaffold,
  waterfall and swimlane commits?

  term maintainability of the package.

  from the real inventory.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● I went through all 32 internal functions (about 1,550 lines in R/). The
  problem is less the number of functions than three patterns that multiply the
  work:

  1. Every argument is written out four times. plot_waterfall() and
     plot_swimlane() repeat each prep_*() argument (13 and 20 of them), pass
     each one along with {{ }}, and document it. Adding one option means four
     edits.
  2. Each prep function repeats the same pipeline. Both prep_*() functions run
     the same steps: resolve columns, check types, drop missing values with a
     warning, check for empty data, check one row per subject, sort, build the
     fill factor, build tooltips. The swimlane runs it a second time for events.
  3. Small features have three separate hand-built versions. Capped labels on
     the waterfall and ongoing arrows on the swimlane each need their own trick
     in ggplot2, plotly and echarts. Those tricks are about 150 lines and the
     most fragile code in the package.

  Smaller issues:
  - The two echarts renderers use two different styles: echarts4r helper
    functions for the waterfall, raw option lists for the swimlane.
  - Colours and shapes go through 7 functions: default_colors, tint,
    default_level_colors, resolve_level_values, resolve_colors, resolve_shapes,
    engine_shapes.
  - Treating "Y" as ongoing is a CDISC assumption built into a package that's
    supposed to be domain-agnostic.
  - The spec object has different fields for each plot.
  - Some tests check internal functions directly (tint(), shape_map), so they
    would break on any refactor.

  Proposed changes

  1. One entry point per plot, and one place that picks the renderer.
  - Titles and axis labels move into prep_*(). plot_*() becomes function(data, 
    ..., engine) that calls render_spec(prep_*(data, ...), engine).
  - Docs are inherited with @inheritDotParams, so each argument is defined only
    once.
  - One list, renderers$waterfall$plotly, replaces the switch() in each plot.
    Adding a plot means one prep function plus three entries in that list.

  2. Switch single-column arguments to data-masking (rlang::eval_tidy(), as in
  aes() and mutate()), and keep tidyselect only for tooltip.
  - Users can pass expressions, for example ongoing = EOSSTT == "ONGOING" or
    value = PCHG * 100. That removes the "Y" special case.
  - Evaluated columns keep their label attribute, so default ADaM axis titles
    still work.
  - Prep builds its standard columns straight from the evaluated vectors,
    without tracking column names.
  - The events table reuses the subject expression, so the "find a column with
    the same name" logic goes away.
  - One eval_arg() replaces resolve_column, quo_is_empty, check_numeric_column
    and column_label.
  - Breaking change: column names as strings ("patient") stop working. You'd
    write .data[["patient"]] instead.

  3. Shared steps written once.
  - drop_missing(), check_one_per_subject() and order_rows() are used by both
    plots.
  - One resolve_scale(values, levels, defaults) handles both colours and shapes,
    and replaces the 7 colour/shape functions.
  - The spec gets a fixed set of fields: plot, data, events, scales, labels,
    ref_lines.

  4. Features I'd drop or simplify (the maintainability trade you offered):
  - Remove waterfall cap. Its labels are the most hacky code in all three
    engines (the invisible echarts markers, the rotated plotly labels, the
    ggplot2 extra headroom). Users can cap values themselves before plotting.
  - Draw ongoing as a marker, not an arrow. Prep turns ongoing subjects into
    extra "Ongoing" event rows at the lane end, drawn with a new right-pointing
    marker shape. They then go through the normal event code, and .arrow_end,
    the arrow-length maths and three custom arrow implementations all go away.
    - Cost: ggplot2 has no right-pointing triangle shape, so it would show a >
      character instead of a drawn arrow.
  - echarts waterfall: rebuild it with raw options like the swimlane, so there's
    one echarts style.

  5. Organise files by plot, not by engine. More plots are likely to come than
  more engines, so each plot would get its own file:
  - waterfall.R and swimlane.R: each holds its prep function and its three
    renderers.
  - spec.R: the spec object, the renderer list and the print method.
  - args.R: evaluating and checking arguments.
  - scales.R: colours and shapes.

  CLAUDE.md's architecture section would be updated to match.

  Result: about 20 internal functions instead of 32, and probably about 30–40%
  less code outside the roxygen docs (a rough estimate). The bigger gain is that
  each concept (argument, validation rule, marker type) lives in one place.

  Order of work

  Each step finishes with the definition of done, so R CMD check has 0 errors
  after every step:
  1. Commit the current code first. Nothing is in git yet, and this refactor
     needs a baseline to diff and roll back to.
  2. Move tests to the public API and the spec, so they survive the refactor.
  3. The spec object, the renderer list, and plot_*() passing arguments on with
     ....
  4. Data-masking and eval_arg().
  5. The shared steps and resolve_scale().
  6. The feature changes: remove cap, ongoing as a marker, the echarts waterfall
     rebuild.
  7. Move code into the new files and update CLAUDE.md.

  Decisions I need from you

  1. Data-masking instead of tidyselect for single columns? This is the breaking
     change for string column names. I recommend yes.
  2. cap: remove it entirely (my recommendation), or keep it with the real value
     shown only in tooltips?
  3. Ongoing: a marker (with the > glyph in ggplot2), or keep the arrows despite
     the extra code?
  4. Checks: copy rlang's standard type-check helpers into the package
     (usethis's use_standalone() for check_string(), check_data_frame() and
     similar)? We wouldn't have to maintain those checks, but the package would
     contain more copied helper functions. Or keep our own 2–3 small checks?

  Once you've answered these and I've committed the baseline, I'll start with
  step 2.

       1 # The spec is the contract between `prep_*()` and the renderers.
          `prep_*()`
       3 #
       4 # * `plot`: plot name, used to pick the renderer.
       5 # * `data`: one row per subject, in plot order, with `.`-prefixe
       6 # * `events`: marker rows (swimlane only), or `NULL`.

       8 # * `labels`: `title`, `x`, `y`, and legend titles (`fill`, `eve

      481 +# Draws one lane as a rectangle from `start` to `end`, so lane
          +s can start at                                                
      482 +# any time (stacked bars can't handle negative starts).       
      483 +swimlane_lane_js <- "function(params, api) {                  
      484 +  var y = api.value(0);                                       
      485 +  var start = api.coord([api.value(1), y]);                   
      486 +  var end = api.coord([api.value(2), y]);                     
      487 +  var height = api.size([0, 1])[1] * 0.7;                     
      488 +  return {                                                    
      489 +    type: 'rect',                                             
      490 +    shape: {                                                  
      491 +      x: start[0],                                            
      492 +      y: start[1] - height / 2,                               
      493 +      width: end[0] - start[0],                               
      494 +      height: height                                          
      495 +    },                                                        
      496 +    style: api.style()                                        
      497 +  };                                                          
      498 +}"

       8  
       9  ## Architecture
      10  
      11 -- `prep_<plot>()`: validates input, does all data manipulation,
         - and returns an                                                
      12 -  engine-agnostic spec.                                        
      13 -- `render_<plot>_<engine>()`: maps a spec to the engine and doe
         -s no data                                                      
      14 -  manipulation.                                                
      15 -- `plot_<plot>()`: exported wrapper that returns the native obj
         -ect (ggplot or                                                 
      16 -  htmlwidget).                                                 
      11 +- `prep_<plot>()`: owns every argument, validates input, does a
         +ll data                                                        
      12 +  manipulation, and returns a spec from `new_floaties_spec()` (
         +fields are                                                     
      13 +  documented in `R/spec.R`).                                   
      14 +- `plot_<plot>(data, ..., engine)`: passes `...` to `prep_<plot
         +>()` and calls                                                 
      15 +  `render_spec()`. Don't add arguments here.                   
      16 +- `render_<plot>_<engine>()`: only reads the spec. No data mani
         +pulation.                                                      
      17 +  Register new renderers in `render_spec()`.                   
      18 +- Files are organised by plot: `R/<plot>.R` holds its prep and 
         +all three                                                      
      19 +  renderers. Shared code: `R/args.R` (argument and data checks)
         +,                                                              
      20 +  `R/scales.R` (colors, shapes), `R/spec.R` (spec, dispatch, to
         +oltips).                                                       
      21 +- New markers go through events and `shape_map`, not engine-spe
         +cific code.                                                    
      22 +- echarts renderers build raw options with `echarts4r::e_list()
         +`.                                                             
      23  - All three engines (ggplot2, plotly, echarts4r) are in Imports
          . Call them as
      24    `pkg::fun()`.
      25  
     ...
      27  
      28  - Follow the tidyverse style guide. Run `styler::style_pkg()` a
          nd get a clean
      29    `lintr::lint_package()`.
      24 -- Tidy eval: `{{ }}` for single columns, tidyselect for multipl
         -e columns,                                                     
      25 -  `.data$` / `.env$` in package code.                          
      26 -- Use `cli::cli_abort()` for errors and `rlang::arg_match()` fo
         -r enumerated                                                   
      27 -  arguments.                                                   
      30 +- Column arguments use tidyselect via `resolve_column()` /     
      31 +  `resolve_columns()`; `.data$` / `.env$` in package code.     
      32 +- Read column labels with `column_labels()` before subsetting r
         +ows.                                                           
      33 +- Argument checks: checkmate `check_*()` wrapped in `assert_arg
         +()`, which                                                     
      34 +  raises `floaties_error_argument`. Data problems use `cli::cli
         +_abort()` with                                                 
      35 +  their own class. Use `rlang::arg_match()` for enumerated argu
         +ments.

● Everything passes. Now I'll compare the size and function count against the
  baseline copy:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The refactor is done. R CMD check passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings and 1 NOTE
  (the same "unable to verify current time" NOTE from this machine). All 150
  tests pass, lint is clean, and I checked all three engines for both plots
  visually.

  What changed
  - One place per argument: plot_waterfall() and plot_swimlane() are now
    three-line functions that pass ... to prep_*(). Titles and axis labels moved
    into prep_*(), so every argument is defined and documented once.
  - One renderer lookup: render_spec() is the only place that picks a renderer
    for a plot and engine.
  - One spec structure: both plots return the same fields: plot, data, events,
    scales, labels, ref_lines.
  - Shared steps: dropping missing values, checking subjects, sorting, and
    colour/shape matching are each one function used by both plots. The colour
    and shape handling went from 7 functions to 4.
  - checkmate for argument checks: they go through one wrapper, assert_arg(),
    which raises floaties_error_argument. That one class replaces six earlier
    ones. Data problems keep their own classes, including a new
    floaties_error_missing_subject.
  - Files by plot: waterfall.R and swimlane.R each hold their prep function and
    three renderers. Shared code lives in args.R, scales.R and spec.R.
  - One echarts style: both echarts renderers now build raw options with
    e_list().
  - Tests now go through the public API and the spec, not internal helpers.
  - CLAUDE.md describes the new layout and rules.

  Size: 1,235 → 949 lines of code (about 23% less), and 32 → 25 internal
  functions. Six of the 25 are the renderers, which have to exist.

  Behaviour changes to know about
  - cap is removed from the waterfall.
  - Ongoing lanes end with an "Ongoing" arrow marker that goes through the
    normal event code. In plotly and echarts it's a clear triangle. In ggplot2
    it's a > character, and at the default size it's small and faint. If your
    outputs don't go to PDF, I could use a Unicode ▶ instead.
  - Bug fixed: ADaM label attributes were lost in tooltip labels. Taking a
    subset of rows drops column attributes, and the labels were read after
    sorting. This bug existed before the refactor. Labels are now read once,
    before any rows change, and a test covers it.
  - Unhelpful message: when the events table has no column matching subject, the
    error is now tidyselect's "Column pid doesn't exist". It no longer suggests
    using event_subject.
  - ggplot2 shapes: they now use numeric point codes, because ggplot2 can't mix
    shape names with a single-character shape. They look the same as before.

  Nothing is committed. The pre-refactor copy is in a temporary scratchpad
  folder, so git has no baseline yet. Should I create a branch and commit, with
  the pre-refactor state first and the refactor as its own commit?
✻ Baked for 13m 2s · done 3:42 PM
